> AGENT

The right solution is to make **VibeComfy the source of truth for a versioned `WorkflowRuntimeContract`**, then make Reigh and Astrid consume that contract instead of reconstructing requirements from route keys, subprocess output, or local conventions.

Today the pieces are scattered: VibeComfy merges `READY_METADATA` / `READY_REQUIREMENTS` into workflow metadata in [ready_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py:33), runtime config comes from `workflow.metadata["comfy_configuration"]` in [session.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:120), Reigh shells out to `vibecomfy run` and adds flags itself in [vibecomfy_adapter.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py:483), and Astrid currently only declares the CLI mapping in [STAGE.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/astrid/packs/external/vibecomfy/STAGE.md:8). That is exactly why SageAttention became bespoke.

**Recommended Shape**
Add a typed contract model in VibeComfy, something like:

```python
WorkflowRuntimeContract(
    version=1,
    workflow_id="video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame",
    source_policy={"runtime_source": "python_ready_template", "raw_json_allowed": False},
    assets={...models, input fixtures, output dirs...},
    nodes={...custom node packs, pins, object_info requirements...},
    python_packages=[...install + verify probes...],
    runtime={...memory_profile, attention_profile, env, comfy flags...},
    inputs={...registered inputs, required fields, override eligibility...},
    outputs={...expected media kind, filename prefix, min bytes, frame/audio expectations...},
    evidence={...gpu, timings, vram peak, commits, validation status...},
)
```

Do not create a second metadata universe. Build it by normalizing the existing VibeComfy fields:

- `READY_METADATA`
- `READY_REQUIREMENTS`
- `model_assets`
- `comfy_configuration`
- registered workflow inputs
- compiled graph output nodes
- runtime metadata written after runs

Then expose it through stable APIs:

```bash
python -m vibecomfy.cli contract inspect <workflow> --json
python -m vibecomfy.cli contract doctor <workflow> --json
python -m vibecomfy.cli contract reconcile <workflow> --apply
python -m vibecomfy.cli contract validate-output <workflow> --metadata out/runs/.../metadata.json
```

**How It Applies**
In **VibeComfy**, `doctor`, `fetch`, `nodes ensure`, `run`, and RunPod validation should all read the same contract. The existing model asset failure path in [session.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:74) becomes one contract check, not a separate runtime surprise.

In **Reigh worker**, route selection stays product-level: `travel_segment__model-ltx2...__profile-default` should map to a VibeComfy template plus task params. It should not encode SageAttention, package installs, or diagnostic runtime params. Reigh should ask VibeComfy for the contract, run reconcile/doctor before queueing, then validate the produced output against the contract instead of falling back through stdout/metadata/candidate file discovery as it does now in [vibecomfy_adapter.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py:2771).

In **Astrid**, `external.vibecomfy.validate` should become contract-backed validation, not just “can this workflow validate locally.” For creative pipelines, Astrid needs the output/evidence contract: expected media kind, paths, duration/frame rules, and whether image/video understanding checks passed.

The first implementation slice should be narrow: make LTX Runexx SageAttention use the generic contract. Move its `runtime_packages` metadata into the typed model, add install/verify probes for `sageattention`, require the runtime flag/env needed for `PathchSageAttentionKJ(auto)`, and make doctor/reconcile/Reigh consume that. Once that works, migrate model assets and output validation into the same contract.

> DEVELOPER

That seems sensible, does it overlap with anything we have selwehre?

> AGENT

I’ll do a targeted overlap scan for existing “contract” surfaces so we can separate reusable pieces from places that would become downstream consumers.

> AGENT

Yes. It overlaps with several existing surfaces, but it should **unify them**, not replace them wholesale.

The strongest overlaps:

- **VibeComfy ready-template metadata**: `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, `model_assets`, `comfy_configuration`. This is the natural seed for the new contract.
- **VibeComfy porting checks**: `port check --strict-ready-template` already has “runtime_contract” diagnostics for missing output contracts, dynamic combo contracts, etc. That should probably become a consumer of the typed contract.
- **VibeComfy memory profile system**: already centralized in `vibecomfy.memory_profile` and documented as the source for worker integrations. Keep that, make it one field inside the runtime contract.
- **Reigh capability contracts**: `scripts/capability_contracts/contracts.json` tracks app-facing route/capability/artifact parity. That is not the same layer. It should reference/consume VibeComfy workflow contracts for implementation readiness.
- **Reigh worker VibeComfy adapter**: currently builds CLI args, handles `--ensure-*`, selects memory profile, finds outputs, and copies runtime artifacts. Much of that should become contract-driven.
- **Astrid artifact/thread contracts**: Astrid already has generic artifact provenance and validation concepts. VibeComfy’s output/evidence contract should feed into those, not duplicate the thread schema.

The clean layering is:

1. **VibeComfy workflow runtime contract**: “Can this workflow run correctly in this environment, and what does it produce?”
2. **Reigh capability/route contract**: “Can this product route satisfy the app/user-visible behavior?”
3. **Astrid artifact/provenance contract**: “What files were produced, how are they validated, and how do they fit into an agent creative run?”

So the risk is not overlap, it is **naming everything contract and letting boundaries blur**. I’d call the new thing specifically `WorkflowRuntimeContract` and keep it owned by VibeComfy. Reigh should not own Python packages, Comfy flags, SageAttention probes, or output filename expectations for a VibeComfy workflow. Reigh should own route selection and product artifact requirements. Astrid should own creative-run provenance and higher-level media validation.

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
```json

> DEVELOPER

any tradeoffs or downsides ehre?

> AGENT

Yes. Main downsides:

- **Contract drift**: if contracts are hand-authored separately from the workflow graph, they become another stale metadata file. Mitigation: infer as much as possible from the built workflow and only require explicit declarations for non-inferable things like SageAttention, system packages, probes, and output expectations.

- **Bureaucracy for new workflows**: authors may need to fill more fields before a workflow runs. Mitigation: support levels: `minimal`, `strict-ready-template`, `production-route`. Local experiments should not need the full production contract.

- **False confidence**: a contract can say “SageAttention installed” or “expects mp4,” but the actual runtime can still fail under GPU pressure. Mitigation: treat the contract as preflight plus evidence schema, not proof of performance. Real RunPod/live evidence still matters.

- **Migration cost**: Reigh currently has a lot of logic around CLI flags, route profiles, output discovery, and capability contracts. Moving to contract consumption will touch multiple repos and tests.

- **Versioning burden**: once Reigh and Astrid consume VibeComfy contracts, breaking shape changes become cross-repo breakages. Mitigation: version the contract from day one and keep compatibility adapters for old fields.

- **Over-centralization**: if the VibeComfy contract grows to include app semantics, it becomes too broad. Keep product concepts like route keys, billing, DB state, and app capability parity in Reigh.

- **Runtime setup side effects**: `contract reconcile --apply` could mutate envs, install packages, or alter node packs. That needs clear dry-run behavior, explicit apply mode, and auditable logs.

- **Hard-to-model dynamic workflows**: Reigh scratchpads patch LoRAs, media inputs, resolutions, output prefixes, etc. The contract must be computed after route/task patching where needed, not only from the base ready template.

The biggest design point: make the contract **derived and validated**, not merely declared. If it becomes a parallel spreadsheet, it will rot.

> DEVELOPER

So wth that in mind, what woudl you suggest?

> AGENT

I’d do it, but as a **thin normalization layer first**, not a big new schema project.

Suggested path:

1. **Add `WorkflowRuntimeContract v1` in VibeComfy only**
   It should be generated from a built `VibeWorkflow`, not authored separately. Start with fields we already have:
   `workflow_id`, `source_policy`, `model_assets`, `custom_nodes`, `python_packages`, `comfy_configuration`, `runtime_flags`, `expected_outputs`.

2. **Make LTX Runexx SageAttention the first proving case**
   Move its current `runtime_packages` metadata into the contract path. Add a generic package probe:
   `sageattention` install source, import check, callable check, required runtime flag/env. This proves the abstraction solves the real failure without designing for every future case upfront.

3. **Add inspect/doctor output before adding mutation**
   Start with:
   ```bash
   python -m vibecomfy.cli contract inspect video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json
   python -m vibecomfy.cli contract doctor video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json
   ```
   Do not add `reconcile --apply` until the read-only contract is useful and stable.

4. **Have Reigh consume only the read-only contract first**
   In the worker/live-test path, replace bespoke checks with:
   - inspect contract
   - fail early if package/runtime/output requirements are unmet
   - preserve product route keys unchanged

   Keep existing `fetch`, `nodes ensure`, and output discovery as fallback during rollout.

5. **Then add output validation**
   Once runs finish, validate `metadata.json` and discovered files against the contract. This catches “process succeeded but no media appeared,” wrong prefix, wrong media kind, tiny/empty files, etc.

6. **Only after that add `reconcile --apply`**
   That command can install declared packages, fetch models, restore node packs, and verify probes. It should default to dry-run.

I would explicitly avoid a huge cross-repo contract migration upfront. The durable version is:

- VibeComfy owns `WorkflowRuntimeContract`.
- Reigh owns route/product capability contracts and references VibeComfy contracts.
- Astrid consumes output/evidence later.
- Contracts are inferred first, declared only where inference is impossible.

The first epic should be: **“Contract-normalize LTX Runexx SageAttention and output expectations.”** If that doesn’t make the current parity path simpler, stop and reassess before widening it.

> DEVELOPER

$megaplan-rubric

> AGENT

Using `megaplan-rubric`, I would **not** run one giant cross-repo megaplan for the whole thing. I’d split it.

For the first implementation sprint:

```bash
megaplan init <brief> --profile thoughtful --depth high --with-prep
```

Shorthand: `thoughtful//high +prep`

Why:

- **Tier**: `thoughtful`, because this is real engineering with cross-cutting consequences, but the first slice can be narrow and reversible.
- **Robustness**: default `standard`; enough process for contract/adapter work without making it heavyweight.
- **Depth**: `high`, because the planner needs to understand VibeComfy metadata, doctor/reconcile, Reigh worker adapter behavior, and current SageAttention/live-test hacks.
- **Prep**: yes, because the planner should explicitly survey existing contract-like surfaces before designing the first schema.

I’d scope the brief as:

> Implement a VibeComfy-owned `WorkflowRuntimeContract v1` read-only normalization layer for LTX Runexx SageAttention and output expectations. Add `contract inspect` and `contract doctor`; migrate current SageAttention metadata/checks into that path; add tests proving it catches uncontracted SageAttention, missing package probe, missing runtime flag, and missing expected output. Do not yet mutate environments or change Reigh production routing.

Then follow-up sprints:

1. **Reigh consumer sprint**: `thoughtful//high +prep`
   Consume `contract inspect/doctor` in live-test/worker preflight, preserving current fallbacks.

2. **Reconcile/apply sprint**: `premium/robust/high` only if it starts mutating prebuilt images, installing packages, or changing production worker startup.

3. **Schema freeze / broad cross-repo adoption**: `super-premium/robust/high` only if we are freezing the contract as a durable wire format that Reigh and Astrid will depend on long-term.

So my suggestion: start with `thoughtful//high +prep`, narrowly scoped, VibeComfy-owned, read-only first. Escalate later only when the contract becomes production-enforced or cross-repo frozen.

> DEVELOPER

Would this ensure we have informaton in each workflow on what it takes to run?

> AGENT

Yes, if we define the acceptance criteria that way.

The key is: every **production / ready workflow** should be able to answer:

```bash
vibecomfy contract inspect <workflow> --json
```

and return enough information to know:

- required model files and loader-facing paths
- required custom node packs
- required Python/system packages and verification probes
- required runtime flags/env/config, including memory and attention profile
- required input fixtures or input media shape
- expected output media kind, path/prefix, and basic validity checks
- source policy: Python ready template vs raw JSON
- last known validation evidence, if any

But I would not require every scratchpad or experiment to hand-author all of that upfront. I’d make the contract **computed** for every workflow, with a readiness level:

- `inferred`: VibeComfy derived what it can from the graph.
- `declared`: workflow adds explicit non-inferable requirements.
- `production_ready`: strict mode passes with output contract and runtime probes.
- `validated`: has recent GPU/live evidence.

So the goal becomes: every workflow has a contract; only app-active / production-ready workflows must have a complete contract.

That avoids bureaucracy while still making the important routes self-describing.

- Add tests proving the contract catches the SageAttention/runtime flag issue.

Do **not** yet:
- mutate environments
- change Reigh worker behavior
- change Astrid behavior
- make all workflows strict
- freeze the cross-repo schema

That gives you immediate value: “what does this workflow need to run?” becomes queryable in one place. It also lets us test whether the abstraction is actually useful before making Reigh or Astrid depend on it.

> DEVELOPER

wait, can we use the deepseek api instead of fireworks

> AGENT

Yes. We can use the DeepSeek API route instead of Fireworks, but not through the stock `thoughtful` profile as-is, because the current canonical DeepSeek/Kimi specs in the rubric point at Fireworks.

Two practical options:

1. **Per-run phase overrides**
   Keep the `thoughtful` profile and override the DeepSeek-backed phases to direct DeepSeek:

   ```bash
   megaplan init \
     --project-dir /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy \
     --idea-file .megaplan/workflow-runtime-contract-brief.md \
     --name workflow-runtime-contract-v1 \
     --profile thoughtful \
     --vendor codex \
     --depth high \
     --with-prep \
     --phase-model prep=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro \
     --phase-model gate=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro \
     --phase-model finalize=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro \
     --phase-model execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
   ```

   Depending on how Hermes is configured locally, the accepted direct spec may be `hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` instead of `hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`.

2. **Create/use a project profile**
   Add a `.megaplan/profiles.toml` profile that is “thoughtful with direct DeepSeek” so we don’t repeat overrides. This is cleaner if you expect to run this pattern often.

For this run, I’d use option 1 unless the CLI rejects the direct spec. If it rejects it, I’ll check the installed Hermes provider names and use the valid direct DeepSeek model string.

> DEVELOPER

let's do that but is there not a shortcut to choose that vendor?

> AGENT

I’ll check Megaplan’s profile/config surface for whether DeepSeek’s provider can be selected globally or via a short flag. If there isn’t one, I’ll use the smallest project-level profile override rather than repeating phase overrides.

> AGENT

There isn’t a `--deepseek-provider` shortcut in `megaplan init`. The built-in shortcut that already exists is profile-level:

- `all-deepseek-pro-direct` = direct DeepSeek API for every phase
- `all-deepseek-pro` = Fireworks DeepSeek for every phase

But for our desired mix, `thoughtful --vendor codex` still uses Fireworks DeepSeek in the mechanical phases by default.

Good news: your user-level profiles already override several profiles to direct DeepSeek. In particular, user `spade-codex` is close:

- plan/revise: `codex:low`
- prep/finalize/execute: `hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`
- critique/review: old Kimi route

However, since we want `thoughtful//high @codex +prep`, the cleanest precise invocation is still:

```bash
megaplan init \
  --project-dir /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy \
  --idea-file .megaplan/workflow-runtime-contract-brief.md \
  --name workflow-runtime-contract-v1 \
  --profile thoughtful \
  --vendor codex \
  --depth high \
  --with-prep \
  --phase-model prep=hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro \
  --phase-model gate=hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro \
  --phase-model finalize=hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro \
  --phase-model execute=hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro \
  --phase-model loop_execute=hermes:deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro
```

If you want a reusable shortcut, I’d add a project profile like `thoughtful-codex-direct-deepseek` in `.megaplan/profiles.toml`. Then future runs can just use:

```bash
--profile thoughtful-codex-direct-deepseek --depth high --with-prep
```

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/README.md\n@@\n - **all-open** — Fireworks-hosted Kimi `kimi-k2p6` for planning/revision, `glm-5.1` for execution and critique (via Hermes)\n - **all-deepseek-pro** — `deepseek-v4-pro` for every phase (via Fireworks)\n+- **all-deepseek-pro-direct** — `deepseek-v4-pro` for every phase (via DeepSeek's direct API)\n - **all-deepseek-flash** — native DeepSeek `deepseek-v4-flash` for every phase (via Hermes)\n - **all-fireworks-deepseek** — Fireworks-hosted DeepSeek for every phase (via Hermes)\n@@\n Inspect with `megaplan config profiles list` and `megaplan config profiles show <name>`.\n+\n+For mixed profiles that include canonical DeepSeek v4-pro slots, add `--deepseek-provider direct` to route those slots through `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` instead of Fireworks.\n \n Model strings take the form `<agent>[:<model>]`. Agents are `claude`, `codex`, or `hermes`. After `hermes:`, a slug with a slash (e.g. `meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b`) routes via OpenRouter; a prefixed direct model (`deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`, `hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/kimi-k2p6`) uses that provider directly; a bare name (`glm-5.1`) uses the matching direct provider. Direct-provider keys live in `~/.hermes/.env`:\n*** End Patch\n"
}

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/docs/megaplan-rubric.md\n@@\n - **`--vendor claude|codex`** — vendor override at tiers 2-4. Defaults to `[defaults].vendor` in `~/.config/megaplan/config.toml` (or `claude` if unset). Tier 1 ignores it (no premium phases); tier 5 silently ignores it (vendor-locked).\n - **`--critic kimi|cross`** — overrides the critique+review pair (preserving the invariant — see below). `kimi` swaps in Kimi for both phases; `cross` swaps to the other premium vendor relative to `--vendor`. Silently ignored at tier 5.\n+- **`--deepseek-provider fireworks|direct`** — swaps canonical DeepSeek v4-pro slots between Fireworks and DeepSeek's direct API. Useful when you want a mixed profile like `thoughtful` but want its mechanical phases to use `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`.\n - **`--with-prep`** — force the `prep` research phase into the workflow regardless of `--robustness`. Off by default; no-op at `robust`/`superrobust`. See \"When to add a prep phase\" above.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

{
  "cmd": "python3 - <<'PY'\nimport json, sqlite3, pathlib\nwanted = {\n    'docs/workflow_onboarding_parity_tooling.md',\n    'ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_lightricks_first_last_parity.py',\n    'tests/test_workflow_lens.py',\n    'vibecomfy/lens/__init__.py',\n    'vibecomfy/lens/core.py',\n    'vibecomfy/contracts/validation.py',\n    'vibecomfy/contracts/ltx_first_last.py',\n}\noutdir = pathlib.Path('/tmp/vibe_recover/extracted'); outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nfound=[]\n\ndef norm(path):\n    p=str(path).replace([REDACTED],'').replace([REDACTED],'')\n    return p\n\ndef handle_tool_calls(raw, source):\n    if not raw: return\n    try:\n        calls=json.loads(raw)\n    except Exception:\n        return\n    if not isinstance(calls, list): return\n    for call in calls:\n        fn=(call.get('function') or {}) if isinstance(call,dict) else {}\n        if fn.get('name') not in {'write_file','create_file'}: continue\n        try:\n            args=json.loads(fn.get('arguments') or '{}')\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n        p=args.get('path') or ''\n        rp=norm(p)\n        if rp in wanted and 'content' in args:\n            dest=outdir / rp\n            dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            dest.write_text(args['content'], encoding='utf-8')\n            found.append((rp, len(args['content']), source))\n\n# scan sqlite messages\nconn=sqlite3.connect('/Users/user_c042661f/.hermes/state.db')\nfor mid, sid, tc in conn.execute(\"select id, session_id, tool_calls from messages where tool_calls like '%write_file%' or tool_calls like '%create_file%'\"):\n    handle_tool_calls(tc, f'sqlite:{sid}:{mid}')\n\n# scan hermes session json files too\nfor path in pathlib.Path('/Users/user_c042661f/.hermes/sessions').glob('session_*.json'):\n    try:\n        data=json.loads(path.read_text())\n    except Exception:\n        continue\n    # recursive walk for tool_calls-like lists\n    def walk(obj):\n        if isinstance(obj, dict):\n            if 'tool_calls' in obj:\n                raw=obj['tool_calls']\n                if isinstance(raw, str): handle_tool_calls(raw, str(path))\n                elif isinstance(raw, list): handle_tool_calls(json.dumps(raw), str(path))\n            # direct function calls sometimes under items\n            if obj.get('type') == 'function' and isinstance(obj.get('function'), dict):\n                handle_tool_calls(json.dumps([obj]), str(path))\n            for v in obj.values(): walk(v)\n        elif isinstance(obj, list):\n            for v in obj: walk(v)\n    walk(data)\n\nprint('\\n'.join(f'{rp}\\t{n}\\t{src}' for rp,n,src in found))\nprint('found unique:', sorted({rp for rp,_,__ in found}))\nPY\nfind /tmp/vibe_recover/extracted -type f -maxdepth 5 -print -exec wc -l {} \\;",

> DEVELOPER

Do we have to aligna ll the other templates with this?

> AGENT

No, we should not force every template to align to the LTX first/last contract.

What should happen:

- **All templates should eventually expose “what it takes to run”**: models, custom nodes, runtime packages, Comfy/runtime settings, named inputs, output expectations.
- **Only LTX first/last templates should satisfy `LTXFirstLastTwoStageContract`** or similar LTX-specific semantic checks.
- Other template families should get their own narrow contracts when useful: WAN I2V, VACE first/last, image edit with mask, upscale, etc.
- Existing templates can keep working without full semantic contracts initially. The pragmatic move is to make the CLI/lens/contract path available, then apply it first to app-active or fragile templates.

So the alignment target is not “make every template look like this one.” It is “make every important workflow inspectable and declarative enough that Reigh/Astrid can know if it can run before burning GPU time.”

The active plan is currently doing that in slices: LTX parity first, then routing/docs/validation around it. I would keep that scope and avoid broad template churn until the pattern proves itself.

and will it apply to all workflows other than ltx images?

> AGENT

Mostly yes, but with two layers:

1. **Generic runtime contract applies to all workflows**
   This is the “what does it take to run?” surface: models, custom nodes, runtime packages, Comfy settings, named inputs, outputs. That should apply across image, video, edit, LTX, WAN, etc.

2. **Semantic contracts apply only where they fit**
   The LTX first/last contract should only apply to LTX first/last video workflows. Image workflows should not be forced into that shape. They should either rely on the generic runtime contract or get their own small image-specific contract later if needed.

So: all workflows should become inspectable for runtime requirements. They should not all become LTX-like.

It’s for `peteromallet/vibecomfy`: **“Systematically replace positional workflow outputs with named handles.”**

My read: this is a real repo-wide issue, not just one LTX cleanup. A quick scan shows many `.out(0)` / `.out(1)` calls across `ready_templates/`, so the ticket is right that this should be fixed in the importer/emitter/doctor path rather than hand-polishing one template.

Best shape:
- preserve Comfy output names from schema/object_info,
- emit `_outputs=(...)` on generated Python nodes,
- generate `.out("name")` where known,
- add a doctor/validation check for avoidable numeric `.out(n)`,
- leave numeric outputs only when names are genuinely unknown.

It overlaps lightly with the workflow-runtime-contract work in spirit, but it’s a separate layer: runtime contracts say what a workflow needs to run; this ticket says the Python workflow source should expose meaningful dataflow handles. The two reinforce each other, but I would keep this as its own VibeComfy epic.

> AGENT

The right approach is to treat this as **schema-backed workflow IR**, then make importer, emitter, doctor, and contracts all consume that same fact.

Core invariant: Comfy runtime still wants numeric output slots. VibeComfy Python should expose **named handles as authoring sugar**, backed by schema metadata, and compile them back to the correct numeric slot.

**For Future Imports**
1. **Schema ingest first**
   Import/convert should always run with the best available schema provider:
   `RuntimeSchemaProvider(object_info)` > local `node_index.json` > source parser.
   
   For each node class, preserve:
   - output slot index
   - output type
   - output name from `output_name`, `output_names`, `RETURN_NAMES`, etc.
   - provenance: runtime object_info, local index, source parse, unknown

2. **Normalize into workflow IR**
   Every imported `VibeNode` should get metadata like:

   ```python
   node.metadata["output_names"] = ["model", "clip", "vae"]
   node.metadata["output_types"] = ["MODEL", "CLIP", "VAE"]
   node.metadata["schema_source"] = "object_info"
   ```

   This is the single source the emitter, validator, lens, and contracts read.

3. **Emitter should use names both places**
   It already emits `_outputs=(...)` when `metadata["output_names"]` exists. The missing systematic piece is incoming edge rendering.

   Instead of:

   ```python
   vae=checkpoint.out(2)
   ```

   emit:

   ```python
   vae=checkpoint.out("vae")
   ```

   when source node slot `2` maps to a stable output name.

4. **Compiler stays numeric**
   `_NodeBuilder.out("vae")` should return `Handle(node_id, output_slot=2, name="vae")`, and `compile("api")` should still produce `["node_id", 2]`. The runtime API should not depend on names.

**For Workflow Contracts**
I would add this to the contract as a **graph/source contract**, not as a runtime install requirement.

Runtime contracts should include enough to prove the workflow is understandable and reproducible:

```json
{
  "graph_contract": {
    "schema_source": "object_info",
    "nodes": [
      {
        "node_id": "103",
        "class_type": "CheckpointLoaderSimple",
        "outputs": [
          {"slot": 0, "name": "model", "type": "MODEL"},
          {"slot": 1, "name": "clip", "type": "CLIP"},
          {"slot": 2, "name": "vae", "type": "VAE"}
        ]
      }
    ],
    "unresolved_positional_outputs": []
  }
}
```

Then `vibecomfy contract doctor` can report:
- **error/warning**: template uses `.out(2)` but schema says slot `2` is `"vae"`;
- **info**: node has no schema, numeric output allowed;
- **error** only for app-active/ready templates if you want to enforce no avoidable numeric handles.

This complements the runtime contract work: runtime contract says “what it takes to run”; graph/source contract says “what this workflow means.”

**For Existing Workflows**
Do this in two passes.

1. **Automated audit**
   Add a command/check:

   ```bash
   vibecomfy workflows doctor --ready-templates --named-outputs
   ```

   Output should name:
   - file
   - node variable if available
   - node id
   - class type
   - numeric slot
   - suggested name
   - confidence/source

2. **Repair generated templates by regeneration**
   For files with the generated marker, don’t AST-patch by hand. Re-run the converter after schema enrichment. That keeps formatting and metadata consistent.

3. **Repair manual templates with an AST-aware fixer**
   For hand-authored files, use a conservative rewrite:
   - only rewrite `some_node.out(0)` where `some_node` can be resolved to a `_node(..., _id="123", _outputs=(...))` binding or schema-backed node id;
   - add `_outputs=(...)` to `_node(...)` calls where known;
   - leave uncertain cases unchanged with diagnostics.

4. **Gate new templates**
   In tests/CI, enforce:
   - generated imports include `_outputs` when schema names exist;
   - emitted edge refs prefer `.out("name")`;
   - ready-template validation finds no avoidable numeric `.out(n)` calls, or only warnings until the backlog is cleaned.

**Tradeoff**
Do not invent names. If object_info/source metadata is absent, numeric slots are honest and should remain. The bad state is not “any `.out(0)` exists”; the bad state is “schema knew slot 0 was `image`, but the emitted Python threw that meaning away.”

My suggestion: make this a VibeComfy epic with three deliverables: schema enrichment in convert, named-edge emission, then doctor/fixer for existing ready templates. Runtime contracts should record and validate the result, but the importer/emitter is where the durable fix belongs.

> AGENT

Yes. The latest `ltx2_3_lightricks_first_last_parity.py` is already on the right path: the dataflow is now mostly readable because it uses named handles like `checkpoint.out("vae")`, `sampled.out("denoised_output")`, and `separated.out("video_latent")`.

The main things I’d clean up next:

1. **Lift repeated constants**
   Lines like seed/model/fps/frames/width/height appear in node creation and again in `register_input`. Define constants near the top:

   ```python
   DEFAULT_WIDTH = 1280
   DEFAULT_HEIGHT = 720
   DEFAULT_FRAMES = 121
   DEFAULT_FPS = 24
   DEFAULT_SEED = 315253765879496
   DISTILLED_FP8_CHECKPOINT = "ltx-2.3-22b-distilled-fp8.safetensors"
   ```

   That makes the workflow easier to scan and prevents drift.

2. **Group the build into readable phases**
   Keep one `build()` function, but add sparse section comments:

   ```python
   # Inputs and runtime primitives
   # Model and encoder loaders
   # First/last image conditioning
   # Latent/audio setup
   # Sampling
   # Decode and save
   ```

   This is one of the few places comments help because the graph is long.

3. **Clean up magic `ManualSigmas`**
   The sigmas string is duplicated as `sigmas=` and `widget_0=` at lines 243-244. That should become:

   ```python
   DISTILLED_SIGMAS = "1., 0.99375, ..."
   ```

   Then pass the constant twice, or better, teach the emitter/schema aliasing that `widget_0` is redundant when `sigmas` is present if that is true for this node.

4. **Prefer named semantic node variables**
   Some variables are good (`first_guide`, `last_guide`, `decoded_video`). A few could be clearer:
   - `prompt` -> `positive_prompt`
   - `negative` -> `negative_prompt`
   - `conditioning` -> `base_conditioning`
   - `concat` -> `av_latent`
   - `sampled` -> `sampled_av_latent`
   - `cropped` -> `cropped_video_latent`

5. **Wrap one-line dense nodes**
   `image_size`, `sampler`, and `SaveVideo` are compact but harder to scan. I’d format them like the others.

6. **Move `_node` helper out of every template long-term**
   For one template it’s fine, but for readability across all ready templates, `_node` should become a shared helper, maybe `vibecomfy.ready.node(...)`, so workflow files are mostly graph code. Existing generated templates all carry this boilerplate.

7. **Registration should reference variables/constants, not raw IDs**
   These lines are still JSON-ish:

   ```python
   wf.register_input("width", "113", "value", value=1280)
   ```

   Better long-term API:

   ```python
   wf.register_node_input("width", width, "value", value=DEFAULT_WIDTH)
   ```

   or:

   ```python
   register_inputs(
       wf,
       prompt=("prompt", positive_prompt, "text"),
       width=("width", width, "value", DEFAULT_WIDTH),
   )
   ```

My recommendation: do the constants + formatting + variable renames now. Then make shared `_node` and variable-based `register_input` part of the systematic importer/emitter cleanup, because those should apply to every future workflow, not just LTX.

> DEVELOPER

So if you were doing a big cleanup of both existing workflows WHILE fixing all instances in future workflows, how would you approach it?

> AGENT

I’d do it as a two-track VibeComfy cleanup: **change the generator first, then mechanically regenerate or repair existing templates**. Otherwise we polish files by hand and the next import reintroduces the mess.

**1. Define the target style**
Set a concrete “readable Python workflow” contract:

- named outputs whenever schema knows them: `checkpoint.out("vae")`
- no avoidable `.out(0)` in ready templates
- named constants for repeated model names, dimensions, seeds, sigmas
- readable variable names, not UUID-ish or generic graph names where inferable
- sparse phase comments for long workflows
- no per-template `_node` boilerplate once shared helper exists
- input registration by builder/node object where possible, not raw id strings

**2. Fix future imports at the source**
Update importer/emitter so every new workflow gets this automatically:

- schema enrichment from `object_info`, `node_index.json`, and source `RETURN_NAMES`
- write `node.metadata["output_names"]` / `output_types` during import
- emit `_outputs=(...)`
- emit `.out("name")` instead of `.out(n)`
- improve variable naming with edge roles and output/input semantics
- emit constants for repeated scalar/string values where safe
- use a shared `_node` helper instead of copying helper code into every template
- produce a “style diagnostics” report alongside conversion

**3. Add doctor checks before bulk edits**
Create a repo-level check:

```bash
vibecomfy workflows doctor --ready-templates --readability
```

It should report:
- avoidable positional outputs
- known output names missing from nodes
- duplicated magic literals worth constantizing
- generated helper boilerplate
- unresolved UUID-ish variable names
- raw node-id registration when a builder variable is available

Start as warnings. After cleanup, promote key checks to errors for ready templates.

**4. Bulk fix existing generated templates**
For generated templates, don’t hand-edit. Re-run conversion through the improved emitter.

That gives consistent:
- `_outputs`
- named `.out("...")`
- formatting
- helper import
- schema-backed metadata

**5. Conservatively repair manual templates**
For manual templates like the LTX parity workflow, use a targeted AST fixer plus human review.

Safe automated rewrites:
- `.out(0)` -> `.out("image")` only when schema/metadata maps slot 0 unambiguously
- add `_outputs=(...)` to `_node(...)` calls when known
- lift exact repeated literals into constants
- wrap dense one-line nodes

Avoid automated semantic renames unless confidence is high. For important workflows, rename variables by hand or through a reviewed patch.

**6. Lock it with tests**
Add tests that fail when the old style comes back:

- converter emits named output handles
- ready-template doctor catches avoidable `.out(n)`
- compile output remains numeric Comfy API links
- runtime contracts include output name/type metadata
- manual templates can still use numeric outputs when schema is unknown

**7. Make contracts record the result**
Runtime contracts should include graph readability metadata, but not own the transformation:

```json
{
  "graph_contract": {
    "named_output_coverage": 0.98,
    "avoidable_positional_outputs": [],
    "schema_sources": ["object_info", "source"]
  }
}
```

That lets Reigh/Astrid know whether a workflow is clean and inspectable without making runtime contracts responsible for codegen.

Execution order I’d use:

1. Add schema-backed output-name enrichment.
2. Update emitter to generate named handles.
3. Add doctor/readability diagnostics.
4. Add shared ready-template helper.
5. Regenerate generated templates.
6. Repair manual templates.
7. Turn the most important diagnostics into CI gates.

That gets both sides: future imports stay clean, and existing workflows move over without hand-maintaining a different style.

> AGENT

All three passes agree on the same shape: this is bigger than named `.out()` handles. The real goal is **ready templates that are Python APIs, not Python-shaped Comfy JSON**.

**Holistic Plan**

1. **Define a Readable Workflow Contract**

A ready template should satisfy:

- no avoidable `.out(0)` when schema gives an output name
- no schema-backed `widget_N` fields
- no UUID class types or `n_<uuid>` variables in checked-in ready templates
- public knobs are registered with `wf.register_input(...)`
- model assets and custom nodes are declared from actual node values
- repeated author knobs are constants: prompt, negative, seed, width, height, frames, fps, steps, cfg, filename prefix
- long workflows have sparse section comments: inputs, loaders, conditioning, sampling, decode/output
- source node IDs remain available for provenance/parity, but are not the primary thing a human reads

2. **Fix the Import/Emitter Pipeline First**

The generator should consume schema during emission, not only validation.

Concrete changes:

- pass `schema_provider` into `emit_ready_template_python`
- enrich every `VibeNode.metadata` with:
  - `output_names`
  - `output_types`
  - widget/input aliases
  - schema source
- emit `.out("vae")`, `.out("image")`, etc.
- resolve `widget_0` to real names wherever schema or `WIDGET_SCHEMA` knows them
- improve variable naming using roles and downstream inputs, not just class names
- hoist obvious repeated literals into constants
- emit real `wf.register_input(...)` calls for user-facing controls

3. **Unify Conversion Surfaces**

Right now `vibecomfy convert` and `port_convert_workflow()` appear to diverge. The readable emitter should be the main path.

I’d make the CLI explicit:

```bash
vibecomfy convert workflow.json --mode scratchpad
vibecomfy convert workflow.json --mode ready-template --ready-id video/foo
```

Both should run the same readability diagnostics before writing.

4. **Add a Readability Doctor Tier**

Add something like:

```bash
vibecomfy workflows doctor --ready-templates --readability
```

It should report:

- avoidable positional `.out(n)`
- unresolved schema-backed `widget_N`
- UUID class types
- `n_<uuid>` variables
- missing `register_input`
- `READY_REQUIREMENTS["models"]` empty while model filenames appear in node values
- `metadata.unbound_inputs` not backed by `workflow.inputs`
- long inline prompts/large literals not hoisted
- generated large templates without sections/constants

Start warnings, then promote the high-confidence ones to CI errors.

5. **Upgrade Contracts**

Contracts should carry enough graph/API info to make templates usable downstream:

- full input bindings, not just names:
  `{name, node_id, field, default, type}`
- output slot/name/type metadata
- named-output coverage
- unresolved positional output count
- unresolved widget count
- model assets inferred from actual aliased fields

This lets Reigh/Astrid understand the workflow surface without scraping Python.

6. **Bulk Clean Existing Templates in Two Buckets**

Generated templates:
- regenerate through the improved emitter
- do not hand-patch unless regeneration cannot represent the workflow

Manual templates:
- apply conservative AST fixes:
  - numeric `.out(n)` to named `.out("name")`
  - add `_outputs`
  - hoist constants
  - wrap dense one-line nodes
  - add missing `register_input`
- review important ones manually, especially app-active video routes

7. **Deal With Opaque/Subgraph Workflows Separately**

UUID class types are not just ugly; they mean the workflow still contains an opaque component/subgraph boundary. Those should block strict ready-template status unless explicitly marked as manual/reference-only.

So the import flow should say:

- scratchpad: allowed with warning
- ready template: fail until expanded/inlined/replaced

8. **Make Public Inputs First-Class**

This was the biggest thing the subagents added beyond our earlier plan.

A template is not easy to use if `set_input("text", "...")` or `set_input("first_image", "...")` does nothing. For every capability, define expected public knobs:

- image/text-to-image: `prompt`, `negative_prompt`, `seed`, `width`, `height`, `steps`, `cfg`, `filename_prefix`
- image edit: source image(s), prompt, seed, strength/denoise if present
- video: prompt, negative, seed, width, height, frames, fps, source images/video/audio
- TTS/audio: text, speaker/reference audio, language, seed, filename prefix

Doctor should warn when the graph has these fields but the workflow does not register them.

**Implementation Order**

1. Schema-backed output names and widget aliases in emitter.
2. Named `.out("...")` emission.
3. Real input binding registration from metadata/schema.
4. Readability doctor.
5. Contract expansion for input/output graph surface.
6. Regenerate generated templates.
7. Manually clean the app-active/manual templates.
8. Turn strict ready-template diagnostics into CI gates.

That gives us a durable cleanup: future imports come in readable, existing generated workflows can be regenerated, and manual workflows get targeted polish without losing parity.

Keep runtime materialization numeric\n+\n+`_NodeBuilder.out(\"vae\")` should return a `Handle` with the numeric slot stored\n+internally. `workflow.compile(\"api\")` should continue to produce Comfy-compatible\n+numeric links. Names are for authoring, diagnostics, contracts, and human/agent\n+understanding.\n+\n+## Existing Workflow Cleanup\n+\n+### Generated templates\n+\n+Generated templates should be regenerated through the improved emitter.\n+\n+Do not hand-edit generated templates except when the generator cannot represent\n+the needed shape. If a generated template requires substantial manual work,\n+change its marker to manual and record why.\n+\n+### Manual templates\n+\n+Manual templates should be repaired conservatively:\n+\n+- replace `.out(n)` with `.out(\"name\")` only when schema or existing metadata\n+  maps the slot unambiguously;\n+- add output names/types to nodes where known;\n+- hoist repeated literals into constants;\n+- add or repair public input registration;\n+- wrap dense one-line nodes;\n+- add sparse section comments to long workflows.\n+\n+Manual semantic renames should be reviewed. They are worthwhile for app-active\n+routes but riskier than output/widget rewrites.\n+\n+## Subgraphs: Practical Policy\n+\n+Subgraphs need two different treatments depending on what they represent.\n+\n+### Opaque imported subgraphs\n+\n+If a workflow import contains a UUID class type or an opaque component node, do\n+not silently promote it to a strict ready template.\n+\n+Policy:\n+\n+- scratchpad import: allowed with a warning;\n+- supplemental/reference ready template: allowed only with an explicit marker and\n+  explanation;\n+- app-active or strict ready template: fail until the subgraph is expanded,\n+  replaced, or wrapped with a named abstraction.\n+\n+The issue is not only readability. Opaque subgraphs hide runtime requirements,\n+inputs, outputs, model files, and failure modes.\n+\n+### Reusable subgraphs as Python functions\n+\n+When an imported subgraph is repeated or semantically meaningful, the practical\n+target should be a Python function/block that mutates a workflow and returns\n+named handles.\n+\n+Example shape:\n+\n+```python\n+def ltx_first_last_guides(\n+    wf: VibeWorkflow,\n+    *,\n+    first_image: Handle,\n+    last_image: Handle,\n+    base_positive: Handle,\n+    base_negative: Handle,\n+    latent: Handle,\n+    vae: Handle,\n+    first_strength: float = 1.0,\n+    last_strength: float = 1.0,\n+) -> Handles:\n+    first_guide = node(\n+        wf,\n+        \"LTXVAddGuide\",\n+        outputs=(\"positive\", \"negative\", \"latent\"),\n+        frame_idx=0,\n+        strength=first_strength,\n+        image=first_image,\n+        latent=latent,\n+        negative=base_negative,\n+        positive=base_positive,\n+        vae=vae,\n+    )\n+    last_guide = node(\n+        wf,\n+        \"LTXVAddGuide\",\n+        outputs=(\"positive\", \"negative\", \"latent\"),\n+        frame_idx=-1,\n+        strength=last_strength,\n+        image=last_image,\n+        latent=first_guide.out(\"latent\"),\n+        negative=first_guide.out(\"negative\"),\n+        positive=first_guide.out(\"positive\"),\n+        vae=vae,\n+    )\n+    return Handles(\n+        positive=last_guide.out(\"positive\"),\n+        negative=last_guide.out(\"negative\"),\n+        latent=last_guide.out(\"latent\"),\n+    )\n+```\n+\n+Use this when:\n+\n+- the same graph fragment appears in multiple templates;\n+- a fragment has stable semantic inputs/outputs;\n+- the function name explains the graph better than individual nodes;\n+- the function can be tested independently by compiling its resulting workflow.\n+\n+Do not force every group box into a function. Some imported groups are visual\n+organization, not reusable abstractions. A good rule: extract only when it makes\n+the calling workflow easier to read and the function has a stable domain name.\n+\n+## Conversion Path Consolidation\n+\n+There should be one canonical conversion implementation.\n+\n+Target:\n+\n+```bash\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out out/scratchpads/<name>.py --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --ready-id <kind>/<name> --out ready_templates/<kind>/<name>.py --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli port check ready_templates/<kind>/<name>.py --strict-ready-template --json\n+```\n+\n+The older `vibecomfy convert` path should be removed or turned into a thin\n+compatibility wrapper over `port_convert_workflow()` after any unique behavior is\n+migrated.\n+\n+Before removal, inventory the old path for useful behavior:\n+\n+- raw workflow loading quirks;\n+- scratchpad compatibility;\n+- API-dict fallback behavior;\n+- tests or fixtures that rely on the older output shape.\n+\n+After consolidation:\n+\n+- docs should point only to `port check` / `port convert`;\n+- the agent skill should describe that path as canonical;\n+- tests should cover the compatibility wrapper or removal error message;\n+- new conversion work should land in one module, not two.\n+\n+## Doctor And CI\n+\n+Add a readability tier to the ready-template doctor.\n+\n+Suggested command:\n+\n+```bash\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows doctor --ready-templates --readability --json\n+```\n+\n+Diagnostics should include:\n+\n+- `avoidable_positional_output`;\n+- `schema_backed_widget_alias_not_resolved`;\n+- `uuid_class_type_in_ready_template`;\n+- `uuid_variable_name`;\n+- `missing_public_input_registration`;\n+- `metadata_unbound_input_not_registered`;\n+- `model_filename_not_declared`;\n+- `large_inline_author_literal`;\n+- `generated_template_has_local_node_helper`;\n+- `large_template_missing_sections`;\n+- `contract_missing_input_binding`;\n+- `contract_missing_output_names`.\n+\n+Rollout:\n+\n+1. warnings only, to establish baseline;\n+2. errors for high-confidence strict-ready issues;\n+3. CI gate for app-active and required templates;\n+4. broader CI gate after generated templates are regenerated.\n+\n+## Runtime Contract Updates\n+\n+The runtime contract should record the workflow's public and graph surface, not\n+only package/runtime requirements.\n+\n+Add:\n+\n+```json\n+{\n+  \"inputs\": [\n+    {\n+      \"name\": \"prompt\",\n+      \"node_id\": \"128\",\n+      \"field\": \"text\",\n+      \"default\": \"The camera moves...\",\n+      \"type\": \"STRING\"\n+    }\n+  ],\n+  \"graph_contract\": {\n+    \"schema_sources\": [\"object_info\", \"source\"],\n+    \"named_output_coverage\": 0.98,\n+    \"unresolved_positional_outputs\": [],\n+    \"unresolved_widgets\": [],\n+    \"nodes\": [\n+      {\n+        \"node_id\": \"127\",\n+        \"class_type\": \"CheckpointLoaderSimple\",\n+        \"outputs\": [\n+          {\"slot\": 0, \"name\": \"model\", \"type\": \"MODEL\"},\n+          {\"slot\": 1, \"name\": \"clip\", \"type\": \"CLIP\"},\n+          {\"slot\": 2, \"name\": \"vae\", \"type\": \"VAE\"}\n+        ]\n+      }\n+    ]\n+  }\n+}\n+```\n+\n+Reigh and Astrid should be able to inspect this contract to understand:\n+\n+- what can be set before a run;\n+- what output artifacts to expect;\n+- whether the workflow source is strict-ready or still a scratchpad/reference\n+  import;\n+- what schema evidence was used to name handles and inputs.\n+\n+## Agent Skill And Documentation Updates\n+\n+The agent-facing skill and human docs should say the same thing:\n+\n+- raw JSON is import material, not the reusable source of truth;\n+- canonical path is `port check` then `port convert`;\n+- strict ready templates require named outputs, named widgets, declared assets,\n+  registered inputs, and no opaque subgraphs;\n+- old conversion commands are deprecated or compatibility wrappers;\n+- subgraphs should be expanded or extracted into named Python functions/blocks\n+  before promotion when they carry semantic workflow logic.\n+\n+Docs to update after implementation:\n+\n+- `README.md`;\n+- `docs/authoring.md`;\n+- `docs/template_porting_workbench.md`;\n+- `docs/adding_templates_models.md`;\n+- `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md`;\n+- any installed Codex/Hermes skill copy, if this repo owns one.\n+\n+## Implementation Sequence\n+\n+1. Inventory conversion paths and decide whether `vibecomfy convert` becomes a\n+   wrapper or is removed with a clear migration error.\n+2. Thread `schema_provider` into the emitter and enrich node metadata before\n+   rendering Python.\n+3. Emit named outputs and schema-backed widget aliases.\n+4. Add semantic variable naming and constant hoisting.\n+5. Add shared ready-template helper functions.\n+6. Emit public input registrations from schema, metadata, and capability rules.\n+7. Expand runtime contracts with input bindings and graph contract fields.\n+8. Add readability doctor diagnostics as warnings.\n+9. Regenerate generated templates.\n+10. Manually repair app-active/manual templates.\n+11. Promote strict-ready diagnostics to errors and CI gates.\n+12. Update docs and agent skill instructions so agents follow the canonical path.\n+\n+## Open Questions\n+\n+- Should source node ids remain the actual `VibeNode.id` for all ready templates,\n+  or should future generated templates use human-readable ids with a separate\n+  source-id provenance map?\n+- Which capability-level input registrations should be required for each media\n+  family?\n+- How strict should we be for supplemental/reference templates compared with\n+  required/app-active templates?\n+- Which imported subgraph patterns are common enough to become first-class\n+  reusable blocks?\n+- Should the readability doctor live under `port check`, `workflows doctor`, or\n+  both with one shared implementation?\n*** End Patch\n"
}

A good rule: extract only when it makes\n the calling workflow easier to read and the function has a stable domain name.\n+\n+For now, preserve actual source node ids as `VibeNode.id` during generation.\n+Moving to semantic node ids is a separate migration that requires a source-id\n+provenance map and stronger parity tests. If that later happens, store the\n+original id in `node.metadata[\"source_node_id\"]` and expose it in contracts.\n \n ## Conversion Path Consolidation\n@@\n Before removal, inventory the old path for useful behavior:\n@@\n - tests or fixtures that rely on the older output shape.\n+\n+Add golden tests before replacement:\n+\n+- old CLI scratchpad output remains importable or receives a clear migration\n+  error;\n+- ready-id behavior is preserved through `port_convert_workflow()`;\n+- API-dict fallback behavior is either preserved or explicitly unsupported;\n+- compatibility wrapper emits a deprecation warning and delegates to the\n+  canonical path.\n@@\n ## Doctor And CI\n \n Add a readability tier to the ready-template doctor.\n \n-Suggested command:\n+Current code has top-level `doctor` and `port check --strict-ready-template`.\n+Either add a new workflow-scoped doctor command intentionally, or implement this\n+as shared diagnostics surfaced by:\n \n ```bash\n-python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows doctor --ready-templates --readability --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor <workflow> --readability --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> --strict-ready-template --json\n ```\n \n Diagnostics should include:\n@@\n - `large_template_missing_sections`;\n - `contract_missing_input_binding`;\n - `contract_missing_output_names`.\n+- `public_input_missing_target`;\n+- `public_input_points_to_missing_field`;\n+- `duplicate_public_input_without_alias_metadata`;\n+- `disconnected_public_input`;\n+- `output_name_collision`;\n+- `output_handle_name_type_mismatch`;\n+- `missing_output_artifact_contract`;\n \n Rollout:\n@@\n ## Runtime Contract Updates\n \n The runtime contract should record the workflow's public and graph surface, not\n only package/runtime requirements.\n \n-Add:\n+This should be additive or a version bump. Current contracts store inputs as\n+`list[str]`; do not break existing consumers without a compatibility path.\n+\n+Add `input_bindings`, `graph_contract`, and output artifact contract fields:\n \n ```json\n {\n-  \"inputs\": [\n+  \"input_bindings\": [\n     {\n       \"name\": \"prompt\",\n       \"node_id\": \"128\",\n@@\n         ]\n       }\n-    ]\n+    ],\n+    \"output_artifacts\": [\n+      {\n+        \"name\": \"video\",\n+        \"node_id\": \"68\",\n+        \"artifact_kind\": \"video\",\n+        \"mime_type\": \"video/mp4\",\n+        \"filename_prefix\": \"output\",\n+        \"expected_cardinality\": \"one\"\n+      }\n+    ]\n   }\n }\n ```\n@@\n ## Agent Skill And Documentation Updates\n@@\n - subgraphs should be expanded or extracted into named Python functions/blocks\n   before promotion when they carry semantic workflow logic.\n+- named `.out(\"...\")` is supported when node metadata contains output names;\n+  older docs saying named outputs are unavailable must be corrected in the same\n+  implementation series.\n \n Docs to update after implementation:\n@@\n ## Implementation Sequence\n \n-1. Inventory conversion paths and decide whether `vibecomfy convert` becomes a\n+1. Baseline inventory: count positional outputs, widget aliases, opaque UUID\n+   classes, local helper copies, missing outputs, and generated/manual/app-active\n+   template categories.\n+2. Add parity harnesses before changing emitters: original API vs regenerated\n+   API, widget-value snapshots, output artifact counts, and representative\n+   image/edit/audio/video fixtures.\n+3. Inventory conversion paths and decide whether `vibecomfy convert` becomes a\n    wrapper or is removed with a clear migration error.\n-2. Thread `schema_provider` into the emitter and enrich node metadata before\n+4. Add golden tests for legacy `convert` behavior and consolidate it into\n+   `port_convert_workflow()` or a deprecation wrapper.\n+5. Thread `schema_provider` into the emitter and enrich node metadata before\n    rendering Python.\n-3. Emit named outputs and schema-backed widget aliases.\n-4. Add semantic variable naming and constant hoisting.\n-5. Add shared ready-template helper functions.\n-6. Emit public input registrations from schema, metadata, and capability rules.\n-7. Expand runtime contracts with input bindings and graph contract fields.\n-8. Add readability doctor diagnostics as warnings.\n-9. Regenerate generated templates.\n-10. Manually repair app-active/manual templates.\n-11. Promote strict-ready diagnostics to errors and CI gates.\n-12. Update docs and agent skill instructions so agents follow the canonical path.\n+6. Emit schema-backed widget aliases and `_outputs=(...)`.\n+7. Emit named `.out(\"...\")` handles where safe.\n+8. Add shared ready-template helper functions before mass regeneration.\n+9. Emit public input registrations from schema, metadata, and capability rules.\n+10. Expand runtime contracts with input bindings, graph contract fields, and\n+    output artifact contracts.\n+11. Update docs and agent skill instructions so agents follow the canonical path.\n*** End Patch\n"

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/docs/readable_ready_template_cleanup_plan.md\n@@\n A strict-ready template should make these questions answerable from inspection\n and contracts:\n@@\n - Is this template app-active/required, supplemental, reference-only, or a\n   scratchpad import?\n \n+Agent happy path:\n+\n+```bash\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows list --ready --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli inspect video/ltx2_3_lightricks_first_last_parity --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli workflows lens video/ltx2_3_lightricks_first_last_parity --json\n+python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor video/ltx2_3_lightricks_first_last_parity --json\n+```\n+\n+The inspection/contract surface should expose enough structured data for an\n+agent to render a form, set inputs, dry-run/compile, and find artifacts without\n+reading generated Python:\n+\n+```json\n+{\n+  \"id\": \"video/example\",\n+  \"capability\": \"first_last_frame_video\",\n+  \"public_inputs\": [{\"name\": \"prompt\", \"type\": \"STRING\", \"default\": \"...\"}],\n+  \"public_outputs\": [{\"name\": \"video\", \"artifact_kind\": \"video\", \"mime_type\": \"video/mp4\"}],\n+  \"models\": [{\"name\": \"model.safetensors\", \"subdir\": \"checkpoints\"}],\n+  \"custom_nodes\": [\"ComfyUI-LTXVideo\"],\n+  \"artifact_paths\": {\"video\": \"out/.../output_00001.mp4\"}\n+}\n+```\n+\n ## Public Input Names\n \n Capability-level input names should be stable. Individual templates may expose\n extra knobs, but they should not invent alternate names for common concepts.\n@@\n Duplicate public names bound to the same node field should be explicit alias\n metadata, not accidental repeated registrations.\n \n+Public input descriptors should carry enough information for agents and apps:\n+\n+```json\n+{\n+  \"name\": \"frames\",\n+  \"type\": \"INT\",\n+  \"required\": false,\n+  \"default\": 121,\n+  \"range\": {\"min\": 1, \"max\": 257, \"step\": 8},\n+  \"aliases\": [\"length\"],\n+  \"target\": {\"node_id\": \"42\", \"field\": \"value\"},\n+  \"media_semantics\": null\n+}\n+```\n+\n+For file inputs, descriptors must state whether values are:\n+\n+- local paths;\n+- URLs to download/stage;\n+- Comfy input filenames already present in the input directory;\n+- references to already-staged assets.\n+\n+Capability-level required inputs should start as advisory diagnostics until\n+templates declare capability, app-active status, primary aliases, and duplicate\n+alias semantics consistently.\n+\n+## Generated Template Anatomy\n+\n+Generated ready templates should have a predictable structure:\n+\n+1. imports;\n+2. constants for public defaults, model filenames, reusable presets, and output\n+   prefixes;\n+3. `READY_METADATA` and `READY_REQUIREMENTS`;\n+4. optional reusable block/helper functions for domain fragments;\n+5. `build()`;\n+6. no local `_node` helper boilerplate.\n+\n+Constants should be selective. Hoist public defaults such as prompt, negative\n+prompt, seed, width, height, frames, fps, cfg, steps, filename prefix, guide\n+strengths, model filenames, and repeated sampler presets. Do not turn every\n+internal enum, one-off numeric setting, or UI-only display string into global\n+constant soup. Drop UI-only display strings when they are safe to remove.\n+\n ## Before / After Target\n@@\n-The desired output is closer to this:\n+The desired output is closer to this complete module skeleton. Loaders\n     checkpoint = ready_node(\n         wf,\n         \"CheckpointLoaderSimple\",\n         source_id=\"127\",\n         outputs=(\"model\", \"clip\", \"vae\"),\n-        ckpt_name=DISTILLED_FP8_CHECKPOINT,\n+        ckpt_name=CHECKPOINT,\n     )\n \n-    # The second review round added important refinements:

- a stricter “excellent generated Python” target, not just prettier graph code
- a generated-template anatomy section: imports, constants, metadata, optional blocks, `build()`, no local `_node`
- a fuller before/after skeleton using proposed helpers: `ready_node`, `bind_input`, `bind_output`, `finalize_ready_template`
- explicit public input descriptors with type/default/range/aliases/media semantics
- public outputs/artifacts as first-class contract fields
- offline-by-default schema policy: live `/object_info` must be opt-in
- atomic regeneration: temp file, validate/parity check, replace only on success
- field validation for `bind_input`, so bad bindings cannot silently write to the wrong place
- additive contract evolution with `public_inputs`, `public_outputs`, and `graph_contract`
- style gates for pragmatic Python quality: no local `_node`, no long one-line nodes, generated formatting checks, public inputs must mutate compiled API
- example-driven acceptance: load, inspect, set inputs, compile, run/dry-run, locate artifacts

I also kept the earlier doc correction in [authoring.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/docs/authoring.md): named `.out("image")` is supported when output metadata is present.

> DEVELOPER

will each 'workflow' be elegant from a code perspective?

> AGENT

If we follow the plan as written now: **much more elegant, yes, but not every workflow should be forced into the same level of elegance.**

The right bar should be tiered:

- **App-active / required ready templates:** should be genuinely elegant Python. Clear constants, sectioned `build()`, semantic names, named handles, explicit public inputs/outputs, no local helper boilerplate, no opaque subgraph junk.
- **Generated supplemental templates:** should be clean and readable, but still allowed to look somewhat mechanical where the graph is huge or weird.
- **Scratchpads / reference imports:** do not need elegance. They need fidelity and diagnostics.

For the “good” tier, the output should feel like a small workflow builder, not a graph dump:

```python
def build() -> VibeWorkflow:
    wf = ready_workflow(READY_METADATA, source_path=__file__)

    # Inputs
    first_image = ready_node(wf, "LoadImage", source_id="31", outputs=("image", "mask"), image="first.png")
    frames = ready_node(wf, "PrimitiveInt", source_id="102", outputs=("value",), value=DEFAULT_FRAMES)

    # Loaders
    checkpoint = ready_node(
        wf,
        "CheckpointLoaderSimple",
        source_id="127",
        outputs=("model", "clip", "vae"),
        ckpt_name=CHECKPOINT,
    )

    # Conditioning / sampling / output
    ...

    finalize_ready_template(wf, READY_METADATA, READY_REQUIREMENTS, source_path=__file__)
    bind_input(wf, "frames", frames, "value", default=DEFAULT_FRAMES)
    bind_output(wf, "video", save_video, artifact_kind="video", filename_prefix=OUTPUT_PREFIX)
    return wf
```

The main caveat: some Comfy workflows are inherently ugly because the underlying graph is ugly: many switches, weird custom-node widgets, visual group leftovers, opaque subgraphs, repeated branches. The cleanup can make those **understandable and safe**, but not always beautiful.

So I’d phrase the goal as:

**Every promoted ready workflow should be readable, inspectable, and maintainable as Python. App-active workflows should also be elegant. Raw imports only need to be faithful and diagnosable until curated.**

> DEVELOPER

But should that stuff be done in code?

> AGENT

Yes, the elegance should mostly be produced by code, not by asking agents to hand-polish every workflow.

I’d split it like this:

- **Generator/emitter owns the default style:** named handles, widget aliases, shared helpers, section comments, constants for obvious public knobs, no local `_node`, formatting.
- **Doctor owns enforcement:** flags positional outputs, unresolved `widget_N`, missing public inputs/outputs, UUID classes, bad variable names, dense one-line nodes, hidden model filenames.
- **Contracts own the public API:** settable inputs, output artifacts, model/custom-node/runtime requirements.
- **Humans/agents only curate the hard cases:** semantic variable names when inference is uncertain, deciding whether a subgraph deserves a reusable function, choosing which weird upstream knobs are public.

So the rule is: **make the correct/elegant shape the automatic output of `port convert`; use manual edits only where judgment is actually needed.**

The practical sequence:

1. Add shared helpers: `ready_node`, `bind_input`, `bind_output`, `finalize_ready_template`.
2. Teach the emitter to use them.
3. Add schema-backed named outputs and widget aliases.
4. Add public input/output inference.
5. Add formatter/style gates.
6. Regenerate generated templates.
7. Manually curate app-active workflows that still need domain judgment.

That way every future workflow starts clean, and existing generated workflows can be improved by regeneration instead of hand editing.

> DEVELOPER

And does the conversation/contracting process seem like it can be highly reliable?

> AGENT

Yes, if we treat “conversion/contracting” as a compiler pipeline with gates, not as an agent judgment call.

The reliable version is:

1. **Import source workflow**
   Preserve source IDs, raw widget values, source hash, and normalized API.

2. **Enrich with schema**
   Offline by default. Use cached/local/source schemas first; live `object_info` only opt-in.

3. **Emit readable Python**
   Named handles and aliases only when unambiguous.

4. **Compile emitted Python back to API**
   Then compare against the original normalized API.

5. **Contract the public surface**
   Inputs, outputs/artifacts, models, custom nodes, runtime packages, runtime flags.

6. **Doctor/strict-ready gates**
   Fail on unresolved schema-backed widgets, opaque subgraphs, missing public controls, hidden model files, output ambiguity, etc.

7. **Atomic write**
   Only replace checked-in templates after import/build/compile/schema/parity checks pass.

That can be highly reliable because every risky transformation has a machine check.

The parts that are less reliable and need explicit fallback:

- custom-node schemas can lie or drift;
- UI widget ordering can differ from runtime schema ordering;
- output names can be blank/duplicated/unstable;
- opaque subgraphs may hide requirements;
- deciding which knobs are “public” is partly semantic.

The plan handles those by falling back to numeric handles, keeping `widget_N` with diagnostics, requiring manual curation for opaque/ambiguous cases, and making public-input rules advisory until a template declares capability/app-active status.

So: **highly reliable for mechanical conversion and contracts; intentionally conservative for semantic cleanup.**

> DEVELOPER

And is there a process an agent should follow to import a workflow and get it to perfection? Like a mix of mecahnical and manual things it should do? Should that be a default included orchestrator?

> AGENT

Yes. There should be a default **workflow import-to-ready orchestrator**. Not one giant magic converter, but a staged command that runs mechanical gates, stops where judgment is needed, and produces a ready-template candidate only when the evidence is clean.

I’d make it something like:

```bash
python -m vibecomfy.cli port ready workflow.json --ready-id video/foo
```

Process:

1. **Preflight**
   Load source, hash it, normalize API, run `port check`, classify blockers.

2. **Schema enrichment**
   Use offline schema sources by default. Optional `--runtime-object-info` for live schema evidence.

3. **Mechanical conversion**
   Emit scratchpad/ready Python with named handles, widget aliases, constants, helpers, and source-id provenance.

4. **Compile parity**
   Import generated Python, build workflow, compile API, compare to normalized source API.

5. **Contract draft**
   Infer models, custom nodes, public inputs, public outputs/artifacts, runtime packages, runtime flags.

6. **Readability doctor**
   Check code quality/readability: unresolved `.out(n)`, `widget_N`, UUID classes, no public inputs, dense generated code, hidden models.

7. **Manual review packet**
   If anything needs judgment, produce a packet:
   - suggested public inputs/outputs
   - ambiguous widgets/output names
   - subgraphs that should become functions
   - model/runtime uncertainties
   - before/after diff
   - exact questions for the agent/human

8. **Curate**
   Agent resolves only the semantic parts: naming, public knobs, subgraph extraction, capability metadata, runtime notes.

9. **Final gates**
   Run strict-ready, contract validate, compile parity, index check, focused tests.

10. **Atomic promote**
   Write ready template only after gates pass; otherwise keep it as scratchpad/out artifact.

So yes: default orchestrator, but **with checkpoints**, not a black box.

The default should be:

- fully automatic for clean workflows;
- conservative for ambiguous workflows;
- explicit about what still requires human/agent judgment;
- safe by default: offline schemas, no RunPod, no overwriting manual files, atomic writes.

That would make “import a workflow and get it to perfection” repeatable instead of depending on one agent’s taste.

> DEVELOPER

And should that be an Astrid orchesrtator?

> AGENT

Probably **no, not primarily**.

This should be a **VibeComfy orchestrator/command**, because the workflow import-to-ready process is VibeComfy’s own compiler/curation pipeline:

```bash
python -m vibecomfy.cli port ready workflow.json --ready-id video/foo
```

It owns:
- schema enrichment
- widget/output aliasing
- Python emission
- compile parity
- ready-template strict checks
- VibeComfy contracts
- template index updates
- generated/manual template safety

Astrid can wrap it later as an **external executor/orchestrator** when the task is part of a broader creative pipeline, but Astrid should not own the core import semantics.

The split I’d use:

- **VibeComfy:** “Turn this Comfy workflow into a clean, validated, reusable VibeComfy ready template.”
- **Astrid:** “Use VibeComfy as one stage in a larger art/video/audio workflow.”

So Astrid might eventually expose:

```bash
python -m astrid executors run external.vibecomfy.import_ready -- ...
```

or an orchestrator that calls VibeComfy import, then runs render/understanding/review. But the source of truth should stay in VibeComfy.

> DEVELOPER

so is that .md doc complete from your perspective? Do you think all that could be done in an ambitous 2 week sprint?

> AGENT

From my perspective, the doc is **complete as a strategic plan**, but not yet as a sprint-ready implementation spec.

It now captures the right shape: generated elegance, canonical conversion, strict-ready gates, public inputs/outputs, subgraph policy, contract evolution, atomic regeneration, and agent ergonomics. What it still needs before execution is task breakdown and scope control.

For an ambitious 2-week sprint: **the whole thing is too much** if “whole thing” includes regenerating/curating all existing templates, contract v2, helper API, doctor gates, docs, and manual app-active cleanup.

A realistic ambitious 2-week slice would be:

1. Implement shared helpers: `ready_node`, `bind_input`, `bind_output`, `finalize_ready_template`.
2. Make conversion atomic and stop writing failed output over targets.
3. Feed schema into emission enough to emit `_outputs` and named `.out("...")`.
4. Resolve schema-backed `widget_N` where safe.
5. Add compile-parity tests for a representative fixture set.
6. Add readability diagnostics as warnings.
7. Regenerate a small representative set: one image, one edit, one audio, one video, plus the LTX first/last path.
8. Update docs/skill for the canonical path.

I would **not** include full repo-wide regeneration, contract v2 downstream integration, all manual template cleanup, or full capability-specific public-input enforcement in that sprint. Those are follow-up sprints.

So: **complete directionally, yes. Two-week sprint, yes if scoped to the vertical slice, no if trying to finish every existing workflow.**

> DEVELOPER

$megaplan-rubric

> AGENT

Using `megaplan-rubric`, I’d run these as **separate megaplan sprints**, not one giant plan.

Recommended profile per sprint:

| Sprint | Profile |
|---|---|
| 1. Safe Compiler Foundation | `thoughtful//high @codex` |
| 2. Schema-Backed Readable Emission | `thoughtful//high @codex` |
| 3. Public Helper And Template Shape | `thoughtful//medium @codex` |
| 4. Public Inputs, Outputs, Contract V2 | `premium/standard/high @codex` |
| 5. Import-To-Ready Orchestrator | `thoughtful//high @codex` |
| 6. Generated Template Regeneration | `basic/standard` or `led/standard @codex` |
| 7. Manual And App-Active Curation | `thoughtful//medium @codex` |
| 8. Strict Gates And Docs Finalization | `thoughtful/standard/medium @codex` |

Most important calls:

- **Sprint 1 should be `thoughtful//high`** because parity/atomic-write safety is cross-cutting and bugs here can corrupt templates.
- **Sprint 4 deserves `premium`** because contract shape becomes a public API consumed by Reigh/Astrid/agents. That’s the highest-stakes architectural piece.
- **Sprint 6 should be cheaper** because once the pipeline is safe, regeneration is mostly mechanical plus batch review.
- I would not use `super-premium` unless Sprint 4 turns into a major architectural dispute about contract semantics across multiple systems.

I would also add `+prep` only if the brief for a sprint depends on unknown external Comfy/object_info behavior. For the current plan, most unknowns are in-code, so default no prep.

If starting with Sprint 1, I’d use:

```bash
megaplan init <brief> --profile thoughtful --depth high --vendor codex
```

For Sprint 4:

```bash
megaplan init <brief> --profile premium --depth high --vendor codex
```

> DEVELOPER

Any decisions you and i should just make now?

> AGENT

Yes. I’d make these now so Sprint 1-4 briefs are crisp:

1. **Legacy `vibecomfy convert`: keep as wrapper for one release**
   It delegates to `port convert`, prints/deprecates clearly, and is removed later.

2. **Schema mode: offline by default**
   Cached/local/source schema only by default. Live `/object_info` requires explicit opt-in.

3. **Source IDs: preserve as actual `VibeNode.id` for now**
   Do not introduce semantic node IDs in this roadmap. Revisit later if source IDs become the main readability problem.

4. **Helper API names: accept the proposed names**
   `vibecomfy.ready_helpers`: `ready_workflow`, `ready_node`, `finalize_ready_template`, `bind_input`, `bind_output`.

5. **Named output safety: conservative**
   Emit `.out("name")` only for unique, non-empty, non-conflicting names. Otherwise keep `.out(n)` with a diagnostic.

6. **Widget aliases: conservative**
   Translate `widget_N` only when schema evidence and parity agree. Otherwise keep positional field plus diagnostic.

7. **Contract evolution: additive**
   Add `public_inputs`, `public_outputs`, `graph_contract`; keep legacy `inputs`.

8. **Opaque subgraphs: mode-specific**
   Scratchpad: warning. Strict-ready/app-active: error.

9. **Manual templates: never overwritten by regeneration**
   Manual repairs are explicit, reviewed actions.

10. **Strict gates: app-active/required first**
   Don’t block all supplemental templates immediately.

The only thing I would not decide solo is the **app-active workflow list** for Sprint 7. That should be a short explicit list from you/Reigh usage, because it decides where manual curation effort goes first.

And number 10, I agree and yeah so can you make these decisions into the document alongside any more questions you have for your ambiguities?
